It’s probably just me being a little tired from work, I don't want to make a huge deal out of it, but... like, I’ll start talking to the nurses about my overnight shift, and then somehow I end up jumping to soccer, or... or paint colors on the wall, and then patient charts. And I can just see everyone getting lost while I'm talking. It's really embarrassing, honestly. I try so hard to be clear and do a good job, I promise.

Um... no, no voices or anything like that. I don't see things that aren't there, I promise. I know that would be really bad, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my mind or that I can't work with the patients.

Um... well... I guess things have been a little tough at home lately. I mean, I'm usually really good about keeping things neat, but... I don't know, I guess I've fallen behind on some things. I keep everything to myself, I promise. \n\nI really hope my answers are okay. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you know I'm still doing a good job at work. Did I... did I answer that right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like things on TV, in the news, or things people say or do, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, I don't think so. I don't really watch a lot of TV anyway, to be honest. Mostly I just come home from the hospital and try to rest so I'm ready for my next shift. \n\nI definitely don't think the news is about me or anything like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, they don't happen all the time, I promise. It's mostly just... after a really bad night, you know? When I haven't slept well or work was really stressful. I just... I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn't wake up. 

It's not like I want to do anything bad, but... sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it. But I always get really scared when that happens! That's why I went to employee health and told someone. Because I know it's wrong to think that way, and I want to be safe. 

I've never really felt this bad before, it's just been lately with everything feeling so heavy. I'm really trying to push past it, though. I know I need to be strong for my patients. I hope... I hope you don't think less of me for having those thoughts. I did the right thing by telling someone, didn't I?

Or have you ever had any unusual beliefs that other people might find strange?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no voices or anything like that. I don't see things that aren't there, I promise. I know that would be really bad, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my mind or that I can't work with the patients. \n\nBut... well, I guess... I don't know if this is what you mean by an unusual belief, and I really hope this doesn't sound paranoid, because I'm usually a very trusting person. But lately, I've just been... well, I'm pretty sure the supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose. And... and I think they're watching me through the unit cameras. \n\nLike, I know the other aides say it's just normal staffing shortages when my schedule gets moved around, and they tell me the cameras are just for patient safety. And I want to believe them, I really do. But it just feels like... like they're specifically watching *me*, to see if I'm being unsafe or doing something wrong. \n\nI try so hard to be perfect on the floor so they won't have any reason to think I'm a bad aide. I really care about my job. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting, I just want to be honest with you. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your head by someone else, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, definitely nothing like that. I've never felt like someone was putting thoughts in my head, or... or that people could hear what I'm thinking. That sounds really scary, honestly. \n\nI mean, I know I just said I feel like the supervisors are watching me on the cameras, but it's not like they're in my head or anything. It's just normal thoughts, you know? Just me worrying about my job and wanting to do well. I definitely don't think my thoughts are being broadcast. I keep everything to myself, I promise. \n\nI really hope my answers are okay. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you know I'm still doing a good job at work. Did I... did I answer that right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like things on TV, in the news, or things people say or do, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, I don't think so. Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think that's pretty normal when you're stressed, right? Like, I used to do a lot more stuff... I'd sketch murals, and I really liked cooking for my friends. We used to play soccer on the weekends, too. 

Lately, though... I don't know, it just feels kind of pointless? I guess I've been staying in my apartment a lot more and ignoring messages. But it's just because I've been so tired from worrying about work, you know? It's not like I'm trying to be a bad friend or anything! I just... I need to rest so I can go back to being a good employee. 

I'm sure once things settle down at the hospital, I'll get back to all my hobbies. It's okay to take a break from things sometimes, isn't it? I really hope I don't sound lazy... I promise I'm usually a really active person.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, definitely not! I mean, that sounds really scary, doesn't it? Having someone else put thoughts in your head? 

I... I don't have anything like that happening. My thoughts are just my own. Mostly just me worrying about work and trying to make sure I'm doing everything right. Sometimes I get a little overwhelmed, but it's just regular stress, I promise.

I really care about my job. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting, I just want to be honest with you. It's just normal thoughts, you know? Just me worrying about my job and wanting to do well. I definitely don't think my thoughts are being broadcast. I keep everything to myself, I promise. \n\nI really hope my answers are okay. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you know I'm still doing a good job at work. Mostly I just come home from the hospital and try to rest so I'm ready for my next shift. \n\nI definitely don't think the news is about me or anything like that. I didn't even mean to do it, it just sort of happened! \n\nAnd sometimes... well, sometimes I'll be getting ready to do my rounds, and I'll just be standing there with my gloves half on, like I'm completely stuck. I know I'm supposed to be checking on everyone, but my body just kind of stops. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know that probably sounds so unprofessional for an aide to do. I really don't want to be a burden on the unit, and I'm trying so hard to keep it together. I know I shouldn't be acting like that. Do you think... do you think that's something we can fix? I just want to do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being taken out of your head or like certain things happening around you have a special hidden meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not like thoughts being taken away or... hidden meanings. Nothing crazy like that, I promise. But... \n\nWell, I do worry sometimes about... about work. I know they say it's just staffing shortages, but... I'm pretty sure the supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose. And... I know this sounds a little paranoid, and I really try not to think about it, but... I feel like they're watching me through the unit cameras. \n\nJust to see if I'm being unsafe, you know? Because of what happened at employee health. I mean, it makes sense that they'd want to keep an eye on me, right? They have to protect the patients. I don't blame them at all! I just... I wish they would trust me a little more. I'm really trying to be careful and do everything right. \n\nI know it's probably just my anxiety acting up, and I shouldn't jump to conclusions. I'm sorry if that sounds bad or if I shouldn't have brought it up. I just want to be honest with you. Is it normal to feel a little watched after something like that happens? I just want to be a good employee again.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events or gestures around you, like people talking, phone calls, or everyday occurrences, were secretly about you or had a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, I don't think so. I mean, I know sometimes people talk in the breakroom and I might wonder if they're talking about me, but... that's just normal insecurity, right? Like, I don't think random things are secretly about me or anything like that. \n\nI know some of the patients on the unit get like that, where they think the TV is talking to them or something, but I promise I don't have anything like that going on. I'm just... I just get a little worried about what people think of me, especially the supervisors. But that's not the same thing, is it? I hope I'm making sense. I really don't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or anything. I'm just trying to be self-aware. I promise I'm really paying attention to your questions. \n\nBut no, if I see people talking or doing things, I don't think there's a secret message in it for me. I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to like?\n\nPatient: Well, um... We used to play soccer on the weekends, too. \n\nLately, though... I don't know, it just feels kind of pointless? I guess I've been staying in my apartment a lot more and ignoring messages. But it's just because I've been so tired from worrying about work, you know? It's not like I'm trying to be a bad friend or anything! I just... I need to rest so I can go back to being a good employee. \n\nI'm sure once things settle down at the hospital, I'll get back to all my hobbies. Oh, no, definitely not. I mean, I listen to the radio on my way to work sometimes, but it's just music, you know? Just regular songs. I'd never think they were about me. I'm just a regular guy, not a celebrity or anything!
